Hello everybody!
I made an account here on Friday, so I guess I should introduce myself.
I first started playing N around the beginning of this year on mofunzone.com (now I know it's an illegal and outdated version (1.2), but I didn't know back then). I finally decided to visit the metanet website around August or September, and it was then I discovered the forums, NUMA, and the latest version of N.
I made an account on NUMA by the same name as here on the 21st of November, and I've submitted a few maps there (mostly DDAs since I like to make them the most). In N, I've currently finished episodes 0-49, 50-54, 60-69, 70-73, 80-81, and 90-95.

Quote: >stardeash, December 29, 2007 02:43 pm
Quote: sirrus, December 29, 2007 03:16 pm
okay, here are some of my ideas.
  • When you hold down on the keypad, N falls into a kind of horizontal sliding posistion for slipping through small gaps



  • When you hold down in mid-air, you also fall into a horizontal 'dive' posistion used for jumping through mines or rolling on contact with the ground.



  • rolling when you hit the ground: The idea is when you dive in mid-air, when you hit the ground from a high jump, you roll kind of like in parkour, making large jumps with a wider landing area survivable.



  • Interactive objects: being able to break through blocks, objects you can throw at enemies or mines to break/explode them, a drone exploding when hitting a mine or a laser, and movable keys and switches are some of my ideas.



  • Trap laser: kind of like a trap door, it would be imbedded in the wall, nearly impossible to see until it charges up and fires when you cross them.



  • Possibley an item that replaces gold in the sense that it gives you more life, the gold would be used in a shop to buy flavours, levels, cheats, and training areas.



Those are some of my suggestions for now, tell me what you think
Honestly i don't really like any of them because it seems that if you were to add those to the game it would just make N into a stereotypical marketed game. Then it would end up being the same like everything else out there. Having N what it is now makes it stick out and differ from other games, even if it's just the games simplicity.
I was just about to say that.
N's unique simplicity is what makes it so fun and great. It's best to keep that simplicity.
Besides, if we could jump through mines, it would also ruin quite a few levels, and they'd have to be redone, and being able to break enemies would make things too easy also, not to mention that the way of the ninja, as described by the story, emphasizes pacifism.

#397017 95 2008-01-05T02:22:28Z PsychoSnail
To prevent the switch from getting stuck on top of the level, when you paste the level data into the box in debug mode, click outside of the box before pressing L to load the data. Otherwise it types an L into the data while loading it, thus screwing up the data.
I posted basically the same thing right above.

#400719 2 2008-01-14T05:40:04Z PsychoSnail
I don't think we're allowed to post completed .sol files (the files in which your save data is stored) here. Sometimes N deletes the .sol file for no apparent reason. It happened to me once, but I had a backup from just a few days before that, so I was fine.
The only thing you can really do is to start over, and make regular backups of the .sol file.
Take a look at .sol Files for Dummies.

Smart Blonde Joke

A lawyer and a blonde are sitting next to each other on a plane.  The blonde is trying to sleep, but the lawyer sees a chance to make some money.  He asks the blond if she'd like to play a game, and keeps bothering her until she agrees to play it.  The lawyer says, "I'll ask you a question and if you get it wrong, you give me $5, but if you can ask me a question I can't answer, I'll give you $500."  So the lawyer asks a very difficult question, and the blonde shrugs and gives him $5.  Then she says, "What has 4 legs when it goes up a hill, 2 legs on top, and 3 legs when it comes back down?" The lawyer tries to think of the answer, contacts all his friends from his laptop to see if they know, but eventually he gives up and gives the blond $500.  As she then tries to go back to sleep, the lawyer exclaims, "Wait, what's the answer?"  The blonde reaches in her pocket, gives the lawyer $5, and goes back to sleep.
Brilliant. I think this one is the best one here so far.

Better
1. Volume controller
2. Music (can be turned off-on)
3. New enemies
4. N chatting (if you have a hard time beating a level, just click on the chat with other people online button)
5. Different doors can lead to different levels ( e.g. one door can lead to Episode 1-3 and another one 2-2)
6. Switches to turn off drones
7. Drone destroying mines (lead drones into a trap!)
8. Doors (not all of course!) stay open for a limited time and when they close you have to go hit the switch99 again...
9. Different sizes of gold (the bigger the more time it profides)
10. Finally, the more levels you complete the better capacities you get (higher jumping, faster running, etc...)
11. Trophees ( when you beet a highscore you get one)


Worse (only 4)
1. Weapons
2. Changing the graphics
3. More keys (I love the simplicity of the game)
4. Making N cost money (NOOOOOO!!!!!!!)
1. It's already in the configuration menu.
2. Play your own music while playing N using iTunes, Windows Media Player, or something like that - it shouldn't cause lag. Besides, putting music would very likely increase the size of the download by a lot, and you probably have interesting enough music (or you could go get some somewhere else).
3. Specifically, what do you have in mind?
4. That's what this forum and the N IRC Chat are for.
5. I prefer going in order. There isn't much point in having numbered episodes and levels if you're just going to skip around all of them.
6. Enemies harm you, and they only harm you. It just doesn't fit N to be able to make enemies harmless (without blocking them with trapdoors, of course).
7. Same as number 6.
8. I like the idea, and I think other people have suggested it before.
9. Put multiple pieces of gold. Works just as well, without making the game too complicated.
10. Would ruin highscoring, as people who have beaten the whole game would have a huge advantage. Also would ruin quite a few levels that are based around the maximum jump hight and running speed.
11. Sounds nice.

By the way, there's an edit button, so you don't have to double and triple post.

1. See #7 below.
2. Zap drones, anyone? Or put floor chasers in the air, though those become bugged. Not bad idea.

#424108 22 2008-04-01T02:29:54Z PsychoSnail
I think you're looking in Underclock mode.
In the Configure menu, make sure the speed for overclocking/underclocking is 0 or above, and then switch the mode to either Highscoring (where time remaining counts as the score) or Speedrun (where the number of frames counts as the score).

Quote: taaveti, April 07, 2008 08:30 pm
If you open userlevels.txt in Notepad, it's under the Format menu. Strictly speaking, you don't need to turn it off, but you're likely to get really annoyed if you try to leave it on.

QFE.

To watch the replay of a map on NUMA, you take the 1.4+ data and paste it in the userlevels box, and then directly after that data you paste the demo data. The demo data only gives which keys were pressed at what times. Without the level data, it's meaningless.

#20085 1 2008-04-26T21:06:27Z PsychoSnail
I tried searching for this before, but didn't come up with a thread similar to this.

Right now on NUMA, you can only see the rating as an approximation, in the form of an image showing whether the map has a rating of 0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2,...,5. As we all know, though, most maps don't have ratings that are an exact multiple of 0.5. As a result of this, what we see as the rating can sometimes be quite a bit off what the real rating of the map is.

It would be nice if next to the image with the ninjas showing the map's rating, we could see the exact rating, or at least a more precise number, such as 4.235, 3.495. Of course, ratings aren't everything, but being able to see a more precise rating would give a better impression of how good a map is, and it would be interesting to see just how highly rated the top-rated maps are, as opposed to seeing a whole bunch of 4.5s.

What do you think?

Edit: Actually, I just found a thread about this same suggestion. Here it is: http://metanet.2.forumer.com/index.php?showtopic=14063

#432950 13 2008-05-01T03:19:27Z PsychoSnail
- Do you find search useful? Why? Why not?

I find search to be very useful, even though most of the time I just click on the links at the side. Search helps me to find certain maps that I am looking for. For example, I searched for the dda "The Hard Way" several times to use as an example while making one of my ddas. It would've taken me a much longer time to find it without the search feature.

- Do you find the suggested search terms at the top of the search page useful?

I find the Race, DDA, and Survival tags to be very useful, but I almost never use any of the other ones. I do like having them there, though, but maybe some of them should be changed so they would be used more often.

- What would encourage you or make it easier to refine your search with more terms?

Boolean searching, especially the "OR" operator, would make it much easier to search for more tags without resulting in 0 results. (See below)

- How else can I improve search to make it easier for you to find the maps you want?

Boolean seaching integrated with the tag system would be very nice, so we could find maps that are DDAs OR N-arts, for example, or all maps that are NOT N-arts.

#433228 4 2008-05-02T03:44:39Z PsychoSnail
Quote: Maligus, May 01, 2008 01:57 pm
I also have a question on this subject. Can you enter your highscores online if you set a highscore on a computer with no internet connection, but you can play N on other computers that do have an internet connection? Please consider that the game is portable, so no progress is lost in the transition.
The progress is actually stored in a .sol file in a separate folder, with all the other .sol files saving progress for other flash games you may have played. So if you want to transfer your progress to another computer, you need to locate that .sol file and copy that (the rest of the game is not needed) - refer to .sol Files for Dummies for more details of how to find it, etc.
But yes, I think transferring your progress to another computer to submit highscores is perfectly fine.

#434901 13 2008-05-10T05:48:31Z PsychoSnail
Quote: Arona Daal, May 09, 2008 10:16 pm
Quote: Kablizzy, April 14, 2008 10:32 pm
It's a different medium. Innovation is the key to just about everything humanity has ever come up with. If we'd never reached out and found a new way to use round rocks, we wouldn't have the wheel.
Why use square rocks as wheels when you can use round ones? N-art is not art innovation. It's a hinderance. It's backwards. I appreciate the time and effort it takes to make a passable N-art but, considering the skill of the best artists out there, you could achieve so much more using paint, which, by today's standards is still basic. Imagine what Dragon_moon could do with Photoshop if he got his hands on a copy.

Art is not the same as wheels. Art doesn't need to be practical; wheels do. Appreciating art isn't all about the final product, like wheels are. Art is also about what went into it, not just what came out of it.

And who knows what Dragon_Moon could do with Photoshop? N-art and Photoshop are two totally different mediums - being good in one doesn't guarantee success in another.

-The second could be an optional "Map Subtype", which would be more like kradda, hard, fast-paced, etc. You could have multiple boxes in the Map Subtype category.

Quote: bhz, April 18, 2008 06:57 pm
You have to download it in a html file...it's 189820 characters long, and the longest word in the English language.
Okay, what you have there is completely useless. It's a sequence of amino acids that may or may not constitute a real protein. The fact that it begins with Methionine suggests that it's real, but you can't be sure it's not all completely random. Regardless, it's definitely not a word. If it is a real protein, it wouldn't be called this; it would be called something like "haemoglobin" or "keratin".
http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titin
It's a real protein, but whether or not it's a real word is still disputed. The simpler name for it is "Titin."

#20331 1 2008-05-25T22:40:28Z PsychoSnail
So, which NUMA do you like more - old or new?
Spoiler: What I thinkPersonally, I like the old version more, for several reasons:

1. The old NUMA captured the simplistic atmosphere of N much better than the new one does in my opinion. I'm not saying that it doesn't look good right now (it looks great), but I prefer the old skin. I'm actually fine with this now.

2. Point ratings - It is now impossible to rate a map 2.5/5 for average. You can only rate in whole-number intervals now. 2/5 is "below average," and 3/5 is "above average." What happened to "average?" Also, people often dock off 0.5 points for a small flaw on a map that doesn't deserve a 5, but is better than a 4. That is now impossible, and I don't like that. Also, the rating displayed on the maps is only by whole number intervals, too.

3. Featured maps - I'm not particularily bugged about this one, but what was wrong with Bitesizing? "Featured Maps" just doesn't sound as nice as "Bitesized Maps," not to mention that "1 Featured Map a Day" limit. I'm fine with this now, too, except for the one-a-day limit. There just may be a flood of great maps one day.

4. No more top-rated page - Sure, there's the "Hot Maps" link, but that doesn't really take the place of it well enough. Classics like "On Route 666" and "Afternoon Dragon 01 Step 04" aren't going to get enough attention now, in my opinion, since now they're almost impossible to find now.

5. The "Hot Maps" page - I like the idea, but now it's kind of hard to tell what maps really are the newest, and people could snipe other maps to make sure their's is on the top. Edit: Also, This places even more of an emphasis on ratings, which people don't like, right? See my posts below for a further explanation.

6. The disappearance of the top-rated authors page - I liked that page (maybe some of you didn't), because it provided links to the most appreciated authors on NUMA, not to mention that I had hopes of reaching that place some day. Oh well.

7. Also, how do you put more than 5 tags on a map?


This isn't to say that I don't like NEWMA. It's still a great place, and I really like some of the new features, such as the "NOT" operator in the search, the avatars, the rennovated profile page, the "Active Authors" page, and the immediate links to previous maps by the author, but I still like the old NUMA more. It would've been great if those could've been added without a change of skin and a complete rennovation, in my opinion.
#439168 5 2008-05-25T23:30:07Z PsychoSnail
Quote: //Zander, May 25, 2008 04:17 pm
I agree profusely...
I thought it would be the same, but with a nice gui, and have the top five maps of today at the bottom of each page... or something like that.
It's not as cool anymore, and is hard to find things and arkward.
It's nice to know that someone agrees with me. The old NUMA was a lot more straight-forward: "Newest" to see the newest maps, "Top-rated" to see the top-rated maps, etc. Now it's a lot harder to find the maps you want to find.

#439183 7 2008-05-26T00:11:31Z PsychoSnail
Quote: wedgie, May 25, 2008 04:53 pm
2.Why are you so bothered about ratings? Everyone knows the many flaws with the rating system. newma is trying to take some of the focus off the ratings and concentrate more on currently popular or active authors.

4. The top rated page barely ever changed, so it was boring to just see the same things all the time. With the new search, things like this would be really easy to find. Imagine a similar map got posted now, it would get loads of attention on the hot maps page, get favourited by many people, then move on. But people would still have it in their favourites. This solves this problem.
2. Taking away the .5 ratings doesn't take off any focus on the ratings. Making it harder to give a fair rating won't make the rating system any better.
4. The top-rated page did change occasionally, though. I had the Top-Rated maps RSS feed before NEWMA came along, and I actually got to see quite a few new maps on there. The maps that were always at the top were there because they deserved to be, and they were really classics. With the hot maps feature instead, people would favorite it and a map would get a lot of attention, but once it disappeared off the page, then almost nobody would know about it.

EDIT: Not to mention that the Hot Maps page places even more emphasis on ratings by determining how long a map can stay on the page by the votes it receives...
#439189 52 2008-05-26T00:27:17Z PsychoSnail
Quote: kkstrong0, May 25, 2008 05:00 pm
One featured map a day isgood for now. The problem with bitesize was that mediocre maps started getting bitesized when many people started to get the abilites. As bitesize manager i tried to limit the number of people who had bitesize accounts, but I didn't want to delete anybodies account, and never found a good reason to take away anyone's powers. NUMA does have lot of quality, but featured maps is only looking for the best of the best of the best.

Quote: YoursTurly, 2008-05-25T22:55:52Z
I don't really like it... no .5s in ratings and the organization overall makes me dizzy

If only you had seen all the hard work that went in behind the scenes, then you would really appreciate newma for what it is. Yes, the .5 thing is frustrating for some, but the reason I like it is becuase it put less emphasis on ratings and more on comments. Comments are what makes a mapmaker great, rating only tell you when you have already some something good, which is hard to achieve for many people.

I don't really like the limit to one featured map a day. It's not really going to help anything in my opinion, and I really think having a review with the maps like it is right now is the only thing necessary to prevent abuse of bitesize/featuring power.

I don't think taking away the .5 ratings is good, because making it harder to give the rating you want to give doesn't take away emphasis from the ratings. I agree that comments are more important, but overall I think NEWMA places more emphasis on ratings than the old NUMA. This is because of the "Hot Maps" feature replacing the "Newest Maps" feature. With the "Hot Maps" feature, the amount of time a map can spend on the page where everybody goes to see maps is determined by the ratings. One could easily get a map off of the page by sniping it a few times. On the old NUMA, the only thing determining the placement was when it was submitted, which makes the list more organized and sensible.

#439236 11 2008-05-26T02:23:26Z PsychoSnail
Quote: Atilla, May 25, 2008 07:07 pm
5. The "Hot Maps" page - I like the idea, but now it's kind of hard to tell what maps really are the newest, and people could snipe other maps to make sure their's is on the top.

Personally, exact chronological order doesn't bother me overly much, as long as one map doesn't stick at the top for ages. I grant you that sniping could be a problem, but it's already somewhat of a problem. So is the large number of poor maps submitted, which few people are interested in playing, and this does something to address that. No net loss, I feel.

Most of your arguments make sense to me, but this one...
The problem is that if poor maps get bumped off the first page quickly, then nobody will ever pay attention to them, and people won't comment on the maps to help the authors get better at making maps. Also, not having exact chronological order makes it feel disorganized to me. I'm sure it doesn't feel that way for some other people, though.

What really bothers me the most, though, is that the majority of people here say that they want to place less of an emphasis on ratings, and also reduce the impact of snipers. The Hot Maps page does the exact opposite of that. Now, snipers can not only reduce the rating of a map, but get it bumped off of the map page where it is most likely to get any attention at all. Now, it is ratings that determine how long a map can stay on the Hot Maps page.

The two things that bother me the most on NEWMA are:
1. The Hot Maps page
2. The lack of .5 ratings (If I think a map deserves a 3.5, why should I be forced to give it a 3/5 or a 4/5? Is it impossible to give an average map a 2.5/5 now?)

[spoiler=This is long! It's from Wikipedia.]Black hole
From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

A black hole is a region of space in which the gravitational field is so powerful that nothing, not even light, can escape its pull after having fallen past its event horizon. The term "Black Hole" comes from the fact that, at a certain point, even electromagnetic radiation (e.g. visible light) is unable to break away from the attraction of these massive objects. This renders the hole's interior invisible or, rather, black like the appearance of space itself.

Despite its interior being invisible, a black hole may reveal its presence through an interaction with matter that lies in orbit outside its event horizon. For example, a black hole may be perceived by tracking the movement of a group of stars that orbit its center. Alternatively, one may observe gas (from a nearby star, for instance) that has been drawn into the black hole. The gas spirals inward, heating up to very high temperatures and emitting large amounts of radiation that can be detected from earthbound and earth-orbiting telescopes.[2][3][4] Such observations have resulted in the general scientific consensus that barring a breakdown of our understanding nature black holes do exist in our universe.[5]

The idea of an object with gravity strong enough to prevent light from escaping was proposed in 1783 by the Reverend John Michell[6], an amateur British astronomer. In 1795, Pierre-Simon Laplace, a French physicist independently came to the same conclusion.[7][8] Black holes, as currently understood, are described by Einstein's general theory of relativity, which he developed in 1916. This theory predicts that when a large enough amount of mass is present in a sufficiently small region of space, all paths through space are warped inwards towards the center of the volume, preventing all matter and radiation within it from escaping.

While general relativity describes a black hole as a region of empty space with a pointlike singularity at the center and an event horizon at the outer edge, the description changes when the effects of quantum mechanics are taken into account. Research on this subject indicates that, rather than holding captured matter forever, black holes may slowly leak a form of thermal energy called Hawking radiation.[9][10][11] However, the final, correct description of black holes, requiring a theory of quantum gravity, is unknown.Contents [hide]
